\displaystyle\left[{\lambda_{\varepsilon}\left({\sum_{s\in\{\pm 1\}}\prod_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}}1-\mathbb{1}\{\mathrm{sign}(x_{n+1},b_{i})\neq s\}\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j})\neq\mathrm{sign}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j},b_{i})\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}]}\right)^{2}\mid Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})>0}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\left({\sum_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}}(1+\lambda_{\varepsilon}({\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,1})=\mathrm{sign}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,1},b_{i})\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}])}\right)^{2}\mid Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})>0}\right]

\displaystyle\leq d(d+1){\mathbb{E}}\left[{\left({1+\lambda_{\varepsilon}({\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{x}_{1,1})=\mathrm{sign}(\boldsymbol{x}_{1,1},b_{1})\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}])}\right)^{2}\mid Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})>0}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4d(d+1)\left({1+{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\lambda_{\varepsilon}({\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{x}_{1,1})=\mathrm{sign}(\boldsymbol{x}_{1,1},b_{1})\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}])^{2}\mid Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})>0}\right]}\right).

(6.34)

Further, Proposition
2.11
implies that for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\lambda_{\varepsilon}({\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{x}_{1,1})=\mathrm{sign}(\boldsymbol{x}_{1,1},b_{1})\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}])^{2}\mid Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})>0}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[{\lambda_{\varepsilon}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\pi_{d,k},1,1})^{2}}\right]+o(1)\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\log^{2}\boldsymbol{\mu}_{\pi_{d,k},1,1}}\right]+o(1).

(6.35)

Finally, the assertion follows from (
6.34
) and (
6.35
).
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

Assume that
0<d<d_{\mathrm{uniq}}(k)
.
For any
\delta>0
there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that for all
\varepsilon_{0}>\varepsilon>0
we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}

\displaystyle\Bigg|{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\log\frac{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime\prime\prime})\vee 1}{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})\vee 1}}\right]

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{E}}\left[{\lambda_{\varepsilon}\left({\sum_{s\in\{\pm 1\}}\prod_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}}1-\mathbb{1}\{\mathrm{sign}(x_{n+1},b_{i})\neq s\}\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j})\neq\mathrm{sign}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j},b_{i})\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}]}\right)^{2}\mid Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})>0}\right]\Bigg|<\delta.

Proof.

Choose small enough
\xi=\xi(d,k,\delta)>\zeta(\xi)>\eta=\eta(\zeta)>\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(\eta)>0
, let
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
, suppose that
n>n_{0}(\varepsilon)
is sufficiently large and let
0<\gamma=\gamma(n)=o(1)
be a sequence that converges to zero slowly.
Let
\mathfrak{E}
be the event that the following conditions occur.

E1:

Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})>0
.

E2:

\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\leq\zeta^{-1}
.

E3:

|\mathcal{X}|=(k-1)\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

E4:

\max_{x\in\mathcal{X},s\in\{\pm 1\}}{\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(x)=s\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}]\leq 1-\eta
.

E5:

\sum_{\tau\in\{\pm 1\}^{\mathcal{X}}}\left|{{\mathbb{P}}[\forall x\in\mathcal{X}:\boldsymbol{\sigma}(x)=\tau(x)\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}]-\prod_{x\in\mathcal{X}}{\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(x)=\tau(x)\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}]}\right|<\gamma

As in the proof of Lemma
6.5
we find that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[{\mathfrak{E}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-2\xi+o(1).

(6.36)

Let

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{L}_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{\varepsilon}\left({\sum_{s\in\{\pm 1\}}\prod_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}}1-\mathbb{1}\{\mathrm{sign}(x_{n+1},b_{i})\neq s\}\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j})\neq\mathrm{sign}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j},b_{i})\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}]}\right)

for brevity.
Combining Proposition
2.7
, Lemma
6.6
and (
6.36
) and using Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left|{(1-\mathbb{1}\mathfrak{E})\log\frac{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime\prime})}{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta/3+o(1),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left|{(1-\mathbb{1}\mathfrak{E})\boldsymbol{L}_{\varepsilon}\mid Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})>0}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta/3+o(1).

(6.37)

Hence, we are left to compare
{\mathbb{E}}\left|{\mathbb{1}\mathfrak{E}\cdot\log\frac{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime\prime})}{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})}}\right|
and
{\mathbb{E}}\left|{\mathbb{1}\mathfrak{E}\cdot\boldsymbol{L}_{\varepsilon}\mid Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})>0}\right|
.
On the event
\mathfrak{E}
we have
Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}),Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime\prime\prime})>0
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\log\frac{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime\prime\prime})\vee 1}{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})\vee 1}

\displaystyle=\log\frac{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime\prime\prime})}{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})}=\log\sum_{\tau\in\{\pm 1\}^{\mathcal{X}\cup\{x_{n+1}\}}}\mathbb{1}\left\{{\tau\models b_{1},\ldots,b_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\right\}{\mathbb{P}}[\forall x\in\mathcal{X}:\boldsymbol{\sigma}(x)=\tau(x)\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=\log\sum_{\tau\in\{\pm 1\}^{\mathcal{X}\cup\{x_{n+1}\}}}\mathbb{1}\left\{{\tau\models b_{1},\ldots,b_{\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}}}\right\}\prod_{x\in\mathcal{X}}{\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\sigma}(x)=\tau(x)\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}]+o(1)

[by
E4, E5
]

\displaystyle=\log\left[{\sum_{s\in\{\pm 1\}}\prod_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}}1-\mathbb{1}\left\{{\mathrm{sign}(x_{n+1},b_{i})\neq s}\right\}\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}\left[{\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j})\neq\mathrm{sign}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j},c_{i})\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}}\right]}\right]+o(1)

[by
E3
].

(6.38)

Now,
E4
guarantees that

\displaystyle\log\left[{\sum_{s\in\{\pm 1\}}\prod_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{\Delta}^{\prime\prime\prime}}1-\mathbb{1}\left\{{\mathrm{sign}(x_{n+1},b_{i})\neq s}\right\}\prod_{j=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}\left[{\boldsymbol{\sigma}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j})\neq\mathrm{sign}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i,j},c_{i})\mid\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime}}\right]}\right]

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{L}_{\varepsilon}.

(6.39)

Therefore, we combine (
6.38
) and (
6.39
) to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left|{\mathbb{1}\mathfrak{E}\left({\log\frac{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime\prime\prime})\vee 1}{Z(\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\prime})\vee 1}-\boldsymbol{L}_{\varepsilon}}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle<\delta/3+o(1).

(6.40)

Finally, the assertion follows from (
6.37
) and (
6.40
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.6
.

Following similar steps as in the proof of Proposition
2.5
, we see that the assertion follows from Lemma
6.7
, Proposition
2.3
and the dominated convergence theorem.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.3
.

Immediate from Fact
2.4
, Proposition
2.5
and Proposition
2.6
.
∎

7.
Proof of Proposition
2.15

7.1.
Proof of Lemma
2.12

The proof is by induction on the height of the tree.
The following claim summarises the main step of the induction.

Claim 7.1
.

For all
\ell\geq 0
, all variables
x
of
{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}
and all
satisfying assignments
\tau\in S({\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)})
we have

\displaystyle\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau,\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))}{Z(\mathbb{T}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))}{Z(\mathbb{T}_{x}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+})}.

(7.1)

Proof.

For boundary variables
x\in\partial^{\ell}\mathfrak{x}
there is nothing to show
because the r.h.s. of (
7.1
) equals one.
Hence, consider a variable
x\in\partial^{2q}\mathfrak{x}
for some
q<\ell
.
If
Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau,\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))=0
, then (
7.1
) is
trivially satisfied. Hence, assume that

Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau,\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))>0
.

Let
a_{1}^{+},\ldots,a_{g}^{+}
be the children (clauses) of
x
with

\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i}^{+})=\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)
. Also let

y_{11},\ldots,y_{1(k-1)},\ldots,y_{g1},\ldots,y_{g(k-1)}
be
the children (variables) of
a_{1}^{+},\ldots,a_{g}^{+}
.
Similarly, let
a_{1}^{-},\ldots,a_{h}^{-}
be the children of
x
with

\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i}^{-})=-\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)
and let

z_{11},\ldots,z_{1(k-1)},\ldots,z_{h1},\ldots,z_{h(k-1)}
be their
children.
We claim that for all
\tau\in S({\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)})
,

\displaystyle Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau,\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{g}\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}Z({\mathbb{T}}_{y_{it}}^{(\ell)},\tau)\right)\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{h}\left(\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}Z({\mathbb{T}}_{z_{jt}}^{(\ell)},\tau)-\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}Z({\mathbb{T}}_{z_{jt}}^{(\ell)},\tau,-\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(z_{jt}))\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(7.2)

\displaystyle Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau,-\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{g}\left(\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}Z({\mathbb{T}}_{y_{it}}^{(\ell)},\tau)-\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}Z({\mathbb{T}}_{y_{it}}^{(\ell)},\tau,\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}_{y_{it}})\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{j=1}^{h}\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}Z({\mathbb{T}}_{z_{jt}}^{(\ell)},\tau)\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.3)

For setting
x
to
\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)
satisfies
a_{1}^{+},\ldots,a_{g}^{+}
; hence,
arbitrary satisfying assignments of the sub-trees
{\mathbb{T}}_{y_{i}t}^{(\ell)}

can be combined, which explains the first product in (
7.2
).
By contrast, upon assigning
x
the value
\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)
we need to ensure
that each of the clauses
a_{1}^{-},\ldots,a_{g}^{-}
are satisfied by at least
one variable other than
x
. This explains the second factor of
(
7.2
).
A similar argument yields (
7.3
).
Dividing (
7.3
) by (
7.2
) and invoking the
induction hypothesis (for
q+1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau,-\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))}{Z(\mathbb{T}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau,\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{g}\left(1-\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{y_{it}}^{(\ell)},\tau,\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(y_{it}))}{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{y_{it}}^{(\ell)},\tau)}\right)\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{h}\left(1-\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{z_{jt}}^{(\ell)},\tau,-\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(z_{i}))}{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{z_{jt}}^{(\ell)},\tau)}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{i=1}^{g}\left(1-\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{y_{it}}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(y_{it}))}{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{y_{it}}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+})}\right)\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{h}\left(1-\prod_{t=1}^{k-1}\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{z_{jt}}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+},-\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(z_{i}))}{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{z_{jt}}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+})}\right)^{-1}=\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+},-\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))}{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x))}\kern 5.0pt,

completing the induction.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.12
.

Applying Claim
7.1
to
x=\mathfrak{x}
completes the proof of Lemma
2.12
.
∎

7.2.
Proof of Lemma
2.14

We employ the
\mathtt{PULP}
algorithm introduced in Section
2.3
and its analysis on the random tree from Section
3.2
.
Recall that given an initial set of literals
\mathcal{L}
,
\mathtt{PULP}
returns a superset
\bar{\mathcal{L}}
with the property that the partial assignment obtained from setting all literals of
\bar{\mathcal{L}}
to true, leaves no clause with only unsatisfying literals.
Let us write
\boldsymbol{N}_{x,s}
for the size of of the output set
\bar{\mathcal{L}}
of
\mathtt{PULP}
initialized with the literal set
\mathcal{L}=\{s\cdot x\}
.

Claim 7.2
.

Let
0\leq t<\ell
and assume that
x\in\partial^{2t}_{\mathbb{T}}\mathfrak{x}
satisfies
\boldsymbol{N}_{x,s}<\ell-t
.
Then for all
\tau\in S(\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)})
and
s\in\{\pm 1\}
,

\displaystyle Z({\mathbb{T}}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau)\leq 2^{\boldsymbol{N}_{x,s}}\cdot Z(\mathbb{T}_{x}^{(\ell)},\tau,s)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.4)

Proof.

Notice that under our assumption on the size of
\boldsymbol{N}_{x,s}
, the assignment
\tau
does not clash with the one imposed by
\mathtt{PULP}
.
The assertion therefore follows immediately from the same argument as in the proof of Lemma
2.8
.
∎

Claim 7.3
.

We have
\lim_{t\to\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\left[{|\partial_{\mathbb{T}}^{2t}\mathfrak{x}|>(200d\cdot(k-1))^{t}}\right]=0
.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
3.2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.14
.

Assume that
\ell>ct^{c}
for a large enough
c=c(d,k)>0
and that
t>t_{0}=t_{0}(d,k)
is sufficiently large.
Then Corollary
3.3
shows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[{\boldsymbol{N}_{\mathfrak{x},\pm 1}\geq t^{c}}\right]\leq\exp(-t^{2})\kern 5.0pt.

(7.5)

Combining Claim
7.3
with (
7.5
) and using the union bound, we obtain a sequence
\varepsilon_{t}\to 0
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[{\forall x\in\partial^{2t}_{\mathbb{T}}\mathfrak{x}:\boldsymbol{N}_{x,\pm 1}<t^{c}}\right]\geq 1-\varepsilon_{t}\kern 5.0pt.

(7.6)

If
x\in\partial_{\mathbb{T}}^{2t}\mathfrak{x}
satisfies
\boldsymbol{N}_{x,\pm 1}<t^{c}
and
\ell>ct^{c}
, then Claim
7.2
yields that for all
x\in\partial^{2t}\mathfrak{x}

\displaystyle\left|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{x}\right|\leq\log\frac{Z(\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{x},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{+})}{Z(\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{x},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{+},+1)}+\log\frac{Z(\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{x},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{+})}{Z(\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{x},\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{+},-1)}\leq\boldsymbol{N}_{x,+1}+\boldsymbol{N}_{x,-1}\leq 2t^{c}\kern 5.0pt.

(7.7)

The result now follows from (
7.6
) and (
7.7
).
∎

7.3.
Proof of Proposition
2.15

We focus on the operator
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{{k},d}
introduced in Section
2.5
.
Let
\rho=\left(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right)
, and

{\rho^{\prime}}=\left({\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right)

be two arbitrary triplets in

\mathcal{P}(-\infty,\infty]\times\mathcal{P}(0,+\infty]\times\mathcal{P}(-\infty,0]
, and write

\hat{\rho}=\left(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right)
and

\hat{\rho}^{\prime}=\left(\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right)
for the
images
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{{k},d}(\rho)
and
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{{k},d}(\rho^{\prime})
, respectively.
We wish to bound
\mathrm{dist}_{d}\left(\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}\right)
in terms of
\mathrm{dist}_{d}\left(\rho,\rho^{\prime}\right)
.

To this end, we begin with bounding the
W_{1}
-distance separately for each of the coordinates
(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}),(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
and
(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}})
. Observe that
it is sufficient to consider only
W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})
and
W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
,
as the triangle inequality implies that
W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}})\leq W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})+W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
.

To spell out our bounds, we need to introduce some additional notation. Recall that for

i,j\geq 1
the random variables
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},i,j}
,
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},i,j}
,
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},i,j}
follow the law of

\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
,
\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}
,
\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}
, respectively. Similarly, let
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},i,j}
,
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},i,j}
,
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},i,j}

be random variables with law
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}
and
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}
,
respectively.
We denote with
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},i,j}
the random variable
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},i,j}\wedge{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},i,j}
, and with
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},i,j}

the random variable
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},i,j}\vee{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},i,j}
. Similarly, we write

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},i,j}={\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},i,j}\wedge{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},i,j}

and
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},i,j}={\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},i,j}\vee{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},i,j}
, and also write

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},i,j}={\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},i,j}\wedge{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},i,j}
and

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},i,j}={\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},i,j},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},i,j}
.

Moreover, for a sign
\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
and a vector
r=(r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}})
of non-negative integers with

r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}+r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}+r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}+r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}={k-1}
and
1\leq i\leq r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
,
1\leq j\leq r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}
,
1\leq\ell\leq r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}
, we let

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}_{i}

\displaystyle(z,r;\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\log{{\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}}\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i-1},z,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i+1},\ldots{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}})\right)\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}})\right)\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}})\right)\right)}}\right|.

Analogously, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}_{j}

\displaystyle(z,r;\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\log{{\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}}\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1},\ldots{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}})\right)\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,j-1},z,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,j+1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}})\right)\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1},\ldots{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}})\right)\right)}}\right|,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}_{\ell}

\displaystyle(z,r;\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\log{{\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}}\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1},\ldots{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}})\right)\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}})\right)\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\ell-1},z,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\ell+1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}})\right)\right)}}\right|.

With the above notation in place, we are now ready to bound
W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})
.
For each of the pairs of distributions
(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}})
,

(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})
, and

(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
, fix an arbitrary coupling among its coordinates.

Lemma 7.4
.

W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{d/2}{1-e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\sum_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1}}\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i}}\!\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}_{i}(w_{i},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};+1){\mathrm{d}}w_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1}}\!\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,j}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,j}}\!\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}_{j}(y_{j},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};+1){\mathrm{d}}y_{j}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1}}\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\ell}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\ell}}\!\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}_{\ell}(z_{\ell},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};+1){\mathrm{d}}z_{\ell}\Biggr]\kern 5.0pt.

(7.8)

Proof.

Let us write
\boldsymbol{\Upxi}_{i,j}^{\prime}(\varepsilon,r)
for the expression
in the r.h.s. of (
2.19
) where distribution
\rho^{\prime}
is used instead of
\rho
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Upxi}_{i,j}^{\prime}(\varepsilon,r)=1-\frac{1}{2^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}}\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon\bigl({{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},4i+j,1}},\ldots,{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},4i+j,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}}}\bigr)\right)\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon\bigl({{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},4i+j,1}},\ldots,{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},4i+j,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}}\bigr)\right)\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon\bigl({{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},4i+j,1}},\ldots,{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},4i+j,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}}\bigr)\right).

(7.9)

By identically coupling the number of clauses and the types of the children variables of each clause in
\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}
,
we see that by the definition of the
W_{1}
norm,

\displaystyle W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}{\left[{\left|-\sum_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{d}_{+}^{\star}}\log{\boldsymbol{\Upxi}_{i,3}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{4i+3})\over\boldsymbol{\Upxi}^{\prime}_{i,3}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{4i+3})}\right|}\right]}\kern 5.0pt.

Applying Wald’s lemma, we further obtain

\displaystyle W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})\leq\frac{d/2}{1-e^{-d/2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}{\left[{\left|\log{\boldsymbol{\Upxi}_{1,3}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{7})\over\boldsymbol{\Upxi}^{\prime}_{1,3}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{7})}\right|}\right]}=\frac{d/2}{1-e^{-d/2}}\cdot\mathbb{E}{\left[{\left|\log{\boldsymbol{\Upxi}_{0,1}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{1})\over\boldsymbol{\Upxi}^{\prime}_{0,1}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{1})}\right|}\right]}\kern 5.0pt.

(7.10)

Let us now focus on the expectation in the r.h.s. of
(
7.10
). Recalling the definition of
\;\boldsymbol{\Upxi}
in (
2.19
), and the definition of
\;\boldsymbol{\Upxi}^{\prime}
in (
7.9
),
we expand

\displaystyle\log{\boldsymbol{\Upxi}_{0,1}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{1})\over\boldsymbol{\Upxi}^{\prime}_{0,1}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{1})}=\log{\frac{1-{2^{-{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}}\cdot\Upgamma\left({{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}},\ldots,{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1}}}\right)\cdot\Upgamma\left({{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}},\ldots,{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1}}}\right)\cdot\Upgamma\left({{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}},\ldots,{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1}}}\right)}{1-{2^{-{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}}\cdot\Upgamma\left({{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}},\ldots,{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1}}}\right)\cdot\Upgamma\left({{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}},\ldots,{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1}}}\right)\cdot\Upgamma\left({{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}},\ldots,{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1}}}\right)}}.

(7.11)

Telescoping over the arguments of the functions
\Upgamma
in the r.h.s of
(
7.11
), invoking the fundamental theorem of calculus for
each term, and applying the triangle inequality we further obtain

\displaystyle\left|\log{\boldsymbol{\Upxi}_{0,1}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{1})\over\boldsymbol{\Upxi}^{\prime}_{0,1}(+1,\boldsymbol{r}_{1})}\right|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1}}\left|\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i}}^{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i}}\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}_{i}(w_{i},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};+1){\mathrm{d}}w_{i}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1}}\left|\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,j}}^{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,j}}\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}_{j}(y_{j},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};+{1}){\mathrm{d}}y_{j}\right|+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1}}\left|\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\ell}}^{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\ell}}\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}_{\ell}(z_{\ell},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};+{1}){\mathrm{d}}z_{\ell}\right|.

Plugging the above into (
7.10
) gives the result.
∎

Following the same steps as above, but replacing ‘
+1
’ with ‘
-1
’, yields the corresponding bound for
W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
.

Lemma 7.5
.

W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{d/2}{1-e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Biggl[\sum_{i=1}^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1}}\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i}}\!\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}_{i}(w_{i},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};-1){\mathrm{d}}w_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1}}\!\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,j}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,j}}\!\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}_{j}(y_{j},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};-1){\mathrm{d}}y_{j}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1}}\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\ell}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,\ell}}\!\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}_{\ell}(z_{\ell},\boldsymbol{r}_{1};-1){\mathrm{d}}z_{\ell}\Biggr]\kern 5.0pt.

(7.12)

Exploiting the signs of the variables with types
\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\oplus$}}
and
\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\ominus$}}
, we obtain the following bounds for each of the
\mathcal{D}
-functions.
For
\lambda\in(0,1]
, we define the real function
\uppsi_{\lambda}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
as

\displaystyle\uppsi_{\lambda}\left(w\right)=\frac{\lambda\cdot w}{1-\lambda\cdot w}\cdot(1-w)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.13)

It is easy to check that
\uppsi_{\lambda^{\prime}}(w)\leq\uppsi_{\lambda}(w)
, for every
\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda
.

Claim 7.6
.

For every

r=\left({r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}\right)
, and

i\in[r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}]
we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}}_{i}\left(w_{i},r;+1\right)\leq\uppsi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1+\tanh({w_{i}}/{2})}{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}}_{i}\left(w_{i},r;-{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\uppsi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1-\tanh({w_{i}}/{2})}{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.14)

Similarly, we also have that for
j\in[r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}]
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}_{j}\left(y_{j},r;+{1}\right)\leq\uppsi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1+\tanh({y_{j}}/{2})}{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}_{j}\left(y_{j},r;-{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\uppsi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-(r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}-1)}}\left(\frac{1-\tanh({y_{j}}/{2})}{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt,

(7.15)

and for
\ell\in[r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}]

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}_{\ell}\left(z_{\ell},r;+{1}\right)\leq\uppsi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-(r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}-1)}}\left(\frac{1+\tanh({z_{\ell}}/{2})}{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}_{\ell}\left(z_{\ell},r;-{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\uppsi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1-\tanh({z_{\ell}}/{2})}{2}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.16)

Proof.

We only prove the first inequality of (
7.14
) as the rest of them follow in a similar manner.
A straightforward calculation shows that for
z\in\mathbb{R}^{q},\varepsilon\in\{\pm 1\}
, and
i\in[q]
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{i}}\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon\cdot z\right)

\displaystyle={\varepsilon}\cdot\frac{1-\tanh(\varepsilon\cdot{z_{i}}/2)}{2}\cdot\Upgamma\left(\varepsilon\cdot z\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.17)

Writing

K={2^{-{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}}\Upgamma\left({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i-1},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,i+1},\ldots{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}}\right)\Upgamma\left({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}\right)\Upgamma\left({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}\right)
,
applying the chain rule, and using (
7.17
), we see that

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}^{{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}}_{i}\left(w_{i},r;+1\right)=\uppsi_{K}\left({\frac{1+\tanh({w_{i}}/{2})}{2}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.18)

Using the fact that
\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}
is supported in
(-\infty,0]
, and that
\Upgamma\leq 1
, we obtain
K\leq 2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}
. The
monotonicity of
\uppsi_{\lambda}
with respect to the parameter
\lambda
concludes the proof.
∎

Using Claim
7.6
, and maximising each of the functions
\uppsi_{\lambda}
appearing in (
7.14
)–(
7.16
), we can recover
the bounds of
[
50
]
. To obtain sharper bounds, a natural idea is to optimise groups of summands, instead of optimising each
\mathcal{D}
-summand of

W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})+W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
in isolation. In particular,
it is tempting to pair terms of the form
\mathcal{D}(\cdot,-1)
with corresponding terms of the form
\mathcal{D}(\cdot,+1)
, as Lemma
7.7
suggests.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
\phi_{\lambda}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
to be the function

\phi_{\lambda}(w)=\uppsi_{\lambda}(w)+\uppsi_{\lambda}(1-w)
.
For every
\lambda\in(0,1]
, we have that
\phi_{\lambda}(w)\leq\phi_{\lambda}(1/2)=\frac{\lambda/2}{1-\lambda/2}
, for all
w\in[0,1]
.

Proof.

For
\lambda=1
, we have that
\uppsi_{\lambda}(w)=w
implying
\phi_{\lambda}(w)=1
, and thus, the result holds trivially. Let now
\lambda\in(0,1)
.
Differentiating
\uppsi_{\lambda}
gives

\displaystyle\uppsi^{\prime}_{\lambda}(w)=\frac{\lambda^{2}w^{2}-2\lambda w+\lambda}{\lambda^{2}w^{2}-2\,\lambda w+1}=1-\frac{1-\lambda}{(1-\lambda w)^{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}_{\lambda}(w)=1-\frac{1-\lambda}{(1-\lambda w)^{2}}-\left(1-\frac{1-\lambda}{(1-\lambda(1-w))^{2}}\right)=\frac{1-\lambda}{(1-\lambda(1-w))^{2}}-\frac{1-\lambda}{(1-\lambda w)^{2}}\kern 5.0pt.

It is straightforward to check that the above expression has only one root at
w=1/2
, being non-negative for
w\in[0,1/2)
, and non-positive for
w\in(1/2,1]
. Therefore,
\phi_{\lambda}(1/2)=\frac{\lambda/2}{1-\lambda/2}
is the maximum value of
\phi_{\lambda}
.
∎

However, directly applying Lemma
7.7
to

W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})+W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})

seems hopeless, since in the bounds supplied by Claim
7.6
, the parameters of the functions
\uppsi
bounding

\mathcal{D}(\cdot,r,+1)
-terms in
W_{1}\left(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\right)

are quite different from the parameters of the functions
\uppsi
bounding the corresponding
\mathcal{D}(\cdot,r,-1)
-terms in

W_{1}\left(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right)
.

The following lemma reveals, a somewhat unexpected, symmetry between
W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})

and
W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
, that facilitates our pairing strategy.

Some additional notation is in order. We denote with
{\mathcal{R}}(k)
for the set of all vectors
r=(r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}})
of
non-negative integer entries which sum to
k-1
. For every
r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)
we use the shorthand

\displaystyle P({r})=\frac{(k-1)!}{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}!{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}!{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}!{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}!}\cdot p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}}p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}\kern 5.0pt,

where
p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}
are the probabilities defined in (
2.18
). Finally, we define

\displaystyle E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P({r})\cdot{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}}\phi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1+\tanh(w/2)}{2}\right)\;{\mathrm{d}}w\right]\kern 5.0pt,

(7.19)

\displaystyle E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P({r})\cdot{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}}\phi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1+\tanh({y}/{2})}{2}\right)\;{\mathrm{d}}y\right]\kern 5.0pt,

(7.20)

\displaystyle E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P({r})\cdot{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}}\phi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1+\tanh({z}/{2})}{2}\right)\;{\mathrm{d}}z\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(7.21)

Lemma 7.8
.

We have that

\displaystyle W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})+W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})\leq\frac{d/2}{1-e^{-d/2}}\left(E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}+E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}+E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.22)

Proof.

Expanding the expectation in (
7.8
) with respect
to
\boldsymbol{r}=\left(\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}\right)
, and using the shorthand

\displaystyle E^{\pm}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[{\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}}\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}_{1}\left(w,r;\pm{1}\right){\mathrm{d}}w}\right],\kern 5.0ptE^{\pm}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}(r)={\mathbb{E}}\left[{\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}}\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}_{1}\left(y,r;\pm{1}\right){\mathrm{d}}y}\right],\kern 5.0ptE^{\pm}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}(r)={\mathbb{E}}\left[{\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}}\mathcal{D}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}_{1}\left(z,r;\pm\boldsymbol{1}\right){\mathrm{d}}z}\right],

we see that

\displaystyle W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d/2}{1-e^{-d/2}}\left({\sum_{r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\cdot E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r)+\sum_{r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\cdot E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}(r)+\sum_{r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\cdot E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}(r)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{d/2}{1-e^{-d/2}}\left({\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\cdot E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\cdot E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}(r)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\cdot E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}(r)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.23)

In a similar manner, we derive

\displaystyle W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})\leq\frac{d/2}{1-e^{-d/2}}\left({\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\cdot E^{-}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\cdot E^{-}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}(r)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\cdot E^{-}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}(r)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.24)

Let us now consider the bound on the sum
W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})+W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
obtained by summing
(
7.3
), (
7.24
). We next group each of the three sums in (
7.3
) with the corresponding sum in (
7.24
), carefully pairing their terms.
Specifically, for the
\mathrel{\raisebox{0.3pt}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
–sums we match the term of
\sum_{r}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\cdot E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r)
corresponding to

r=({r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}})
with the term of
\sum_{r^{\prime}}P(r^{\prime})\cdot r^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\cdot E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r^{\prime})
that corresponds to

r^{\prime}=({r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}})
. Since
r\mapsto r^{\prime}
is a bijection of

\mathcal{R}(k)\cap\{r:r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\geq 1\}
, and
r^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}=r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
, and
P(r^{\prime})=P(r)
we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}\geq 1}\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\left({E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r)+E^{-}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r)}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}\geq 1}\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\left({E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r)+E^{-}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(r^{\prime})}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.25)

Invoking the bounds (
7.14
) of Claim
7.6
, and recalling the definitions of
\phi
,
E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
, we upper bound the r.h.s. of
(
7.25
) by

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}\geq 1}\end{subarray}}P(r)r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\left({\mathbb{E}\left[{\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}}\uppsi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1+\tanh(w/2)}{2}\right)\;{\mathrm{d}}w}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\int_{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\wedge}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}}^{{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}^{\vee}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}}\uppsi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1-\tanh(w/2)}{2}\right)\;{\mathrm{d}}w}\right]}\right)=E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\kern 5.0pt.

(7.26)

The matchings between the terms for the
\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\oplus$}},\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\ominus$}}
–sums of (
7.3
), (
7.24
) are more delicate.
In particular, for the
\mathrel{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\oplus$}}
–sum it turns out that we can pull off the same trick as above by pairing the term of

\sum_{r}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\cdot E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}(r)
corresponding to the vector
r=({r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}})
with the term of
\sum_{r^{\prime\prime}}P(r^{\prime\prime})\cdot r^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\cdot E^{-}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}(r^{\prime\prime})
that corresponds to

r^{\prime\prime}=({r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}+1,{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}-1,{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}})
. To see this, note that the mapping
r\mapsto r^{\prime\prime}
is a bijection of

\mathcal{R}(k)\cap\{r:r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\geq 1\}
, leaving the quantity
P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}
invariant as

\displaystyle P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}=\frac{(k-1)!}{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}!({r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}-1)!{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}!{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}!}\cdot p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}}p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}=\frac{(k-1)!}{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}!({r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}+1)!({r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}-1)!{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}!}\cdot p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}}p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}}p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}^{{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}\cdot(r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}+1)=P(r^{\prime\prime})\cdot r^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\kern 5.0pt.

Invoking the bounds (
7.15
) of Claim
7.6
, recalling the definitions of
\phi
,
E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}
, and arguing as above,
we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\left({E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}(r)+E^{-}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}(r^{\prime\prime})}\right)\leq E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\kern 5.0pt.

(7.27)

Similarly, using the mapping
r\mapsto r^{\prime\prime\prime}
, with
r^{\prime\prime\prime}\!=(r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}-1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}+1,r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}})
, and following the same steps as above, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\in{\mathcal{R}}(k)\\
r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\geq 1\end{subarray}}P(r)\cdot r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\left({E^{+}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}(r)+E^{-}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}(r^{\prime\prime\prime})}\right)\leq E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\kern 5.0pt.

(7.28)

Summing (
7.26
)–(
7.28
) concludes the proof.
∎

In light of the above, we are now ready to finish the proof of Proposition
2.15
.

Proof of Proposition
2.15
.

Applying Lemma
7.7
on the function
\phi
in the r.h.s. of (
7.19
) gives

\displaystyle\phi_{2^{-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-r_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}}}\left(\frac{1+\tanh(w/2)}{2}\right)\leq\frac{2^{-\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}-\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-1}}{1-2^{-\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}-\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}-1}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}+\boldsymbol{r}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

(7.29)

Plugging the above into (
7.19
) and applying the binomial theorem, further yields

\displaystyle E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\leq(k-1)\cdot p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\left({1-\frac{e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}{2}}\right)^{k-2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}-{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}|}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(7.30)

Working in a similar manner, we obtain

\displaystyle E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\leq(k-1)\cdot p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\left(1-\frac{e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\right)^{k-2}\!\!{\mathbb{E}}\left[{|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}-{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}|}\right],

and

\displaystyle E_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\leq(k-1)\cdot p_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\left(1-\frac{e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\right)^{k-2}\!\!{\mathbb{E}}\left[{|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}-{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}|}\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(7.31)

Finally, plugging the bounds (
7.30
),
and (
7.31
) into (
7.22
) we see that

W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})+W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})

is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{d\cdot(k-1)}{2}\cdot\left(1-\frac{e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\right)^{k-2}\!\!\left[\left(1-e^{-\frac{d}{2}}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[{|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}-{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},1,1}|}\right]+e^{-\frac{d}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}-{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},1,1}|}\right]+e^{-\frac{d}{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[{|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}-{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},1,1}|}\right]\right]\kern 5.0pt.

(7.32)

Recall that we established (
7.32
) assuming an arbitrary coupling between the coordinates of each pair of distributions
(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}})
,

(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})
, and
(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})
. Therefore, the definition of
W_{1}
norm and (
7.32
), imply the first inequality
below, while (
7.33
) follows by the definition (
2.22
) of
\mathrm{dist}_{d}

\displaystyle W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})+W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d(k-1)}{2}\left(1-\frac{e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\right)^{k-2}\left[\left(1-e^{-\frac{d}{2}}\right)W_{1}(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}})+e^{-\frac{d}{2}}W_{1}(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})+e^{-\frac{d}{2}}W_{1}(\rho_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\rho^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d(k-1)}{2}\left(1-\frac{e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\right)^{k-2}\mathrm{dist}_{d}(\rho,\rho^{\prime})\kern 5.0pt.

(7.33)

Moreover, as per the triangle inequality we see that

\displaystyle W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}})\leq W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}})+W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}})\leq\frac{d(k-1)}{2}\left(1-\frac{e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\right)^{k-2}\mathrm{dist}_{d}(\rho,\rho^{\prime})\kern 5.0pt.

(7.34)

Plugging the bounds (
7.33
) and (
7.34
) into the expression of
\mathrm{dist}_{d}\left({\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}\right)
yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}_{d}\left({\hat{\rho},\hat{\rho}^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=(1-e^{-d/2})\cdot W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}^{\prime})+e^{-d/2}\left({W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}^{\prime})+W_{1}(\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}},\hat{\rho}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}^{\prime})}\right)\leq\frac{d(k-1)}{2}\left(1-\frac{e^{-\frac{d}{2}}}{2}\right)^{k-2}\mathrm{dist}_{d}(\rho,\rho^{\prime})\kern 5.0pt.

Recalling the definition of
{d_{\mathrm{con}}}
, we see that for
d<d_{\mathrm{con}}(k)
, the operator
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{k,d}

contracts with respect to the metric
\mathrm{dist}_{d}
, as desired.
∎

7.4.
Proof of Proposition
2.13

To get a handle on the
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{x}^{(\ell)}
from (
2.12
), we show that these quantities
can be calculated by propagating the extremal boundary condition
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{+}

bottom-up toward the root of the tree. Specifically, we consider the operator

\displaystyle\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}

\displaystyle:(-\infty,\infty]^{V({\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)})}\to(-\infty,\infty]^{V({\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)})}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle\mapsto\hat{\eta}=\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}(\eta)\kern 5.0pt,

defined as follows.
For all
x\in\partial^{2\ell}\mathfrak{x}
we set
\hat{\eta}_{x}=\infty
.
Moreover, for a variable
x\in\partial^{2q}\mathfrak{x}
with
q<\ell
having children clauses
a_{1},\ldots,a_{t}
, and grandchildren variables
y_{1,1},\ldots,y_{1,(k-1)},\ldots,y_{t,1},\ldots,y_{t,(k-1)}
we define

\displaystyle\hat{\eta}_{x}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{t}{\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)}\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i})\cdot\log\left(1-{\Upgamma\left({\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)}\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i})\cdot(\eta_{y_{i,1}},\ldots,\eta_{y_{1,(k-1)}})\right)}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.35)

It may not be apparent that the sum above is well-defined as
-\infty
summands may manifest.
The following lemma rules out such possibility and shows that the
\ell
-fold iteration of
\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}
, initiated all-
(+\infty)
yields
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}=({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{x})_{x\in V({\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)})}
.

Lemma 7.9
.

The operator
\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}
is well-defined and

\mathrm{LL}^{+\,(t)}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}(+\infty,\ldots,+\infty)={\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}

for every
t\geq\ell
.

Proof.

To show that
\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}
is well defined we verify that, in the notation of (
7.35
),

\hat{\eta}_{x}\in(-\infty,\infty]
for all
x
. Indeed, in the expression on the r.h.s. of (
7.35
) a

\pm\infty
summand can arise only from variables
y_{i,j}
with
\eta_{y_{i,j}}=\infty
. But the definition of

\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}
ensures that such
y_{i,j}
either render a zero summand if
\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i})=-1
, or a
+\infty
summand if
\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i})=1
.
Thus, the sum is well-defined and
\hat{\eta}_{x}\in(-\infty,\infty]
.

Further, to verify the identity
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}=\mathrm{LL}^{+\,(\ell)}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}(\infty,\ldots,\infty)
, consider a variable
x
of
\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}
.
Let
a_{1}^{+},\ldots,a_{g}^{+}
be the children (clauses) of
x
with
\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i}^{+})=\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)
. Also let
y_{11},\ldots,y_{1(k-1)},\ldots,y_{g1},\ldots,y_{g(k-1)}
be the children of
a_{1}^{+},\ldots,a_{g}^{+}
.
Similarly, let
a_{1}^{-},\ldots,a_{h}^{-}
be the children of
x
with

\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i}^{-})=-\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)
and let

z_{11},\ldots,z_{1(k-1)},\ldots,z_{h1},\ldots,z_{h(k-1)}
be their
children. Then (
7.2
), and (
7.3
) yield

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{x}^{(\ell)}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{g}\log\left(1-\prod_{q=1}^{k-1}\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{y_{iq}}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}({y_{iq}}))}{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{y_{iq}}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+})}\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{h}\log\left(1-\prod_{q=1}^{k-1}\frac{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{z_{jq}}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+},-\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}({z_{jq}}))}{Z({\mathbb{T}}_{z_{jq}}^{(\ell)},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+})}\right)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i=1}^{g}\log\left(1-\Upgamma\left(\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i}^{+})\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)\cdot\left({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{y_{i1}},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{y_{i(k-1)}}\right)\right)\right)+\sum_{j=1}^{h}\log\left(1-\Upgamma\left(\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i}^{-})\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)\cdot\left({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{z_{j1}},\ldots,{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{z_{j(k-1)}}\right)\right)\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.36)

The assertion follows because
\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i}^{+})\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)=1
and
\mathrm{sign}(x,a_{i}^{-})\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(x)=-1
.
∎

The next aim is to approximate the
\ell
-fold iteration of
\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}
, and more specifically the distribution of
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{x}}^{(\ell)}
, using a non-random operator.
To this end, we need to cope with the
\pm\infty
-entries of the vector
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}
.
This is addressed by Lemma
2.14
, proven in Section
7.2
, which provides a bound on
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{x}
for variables
x
near the root of the tree.

In the following we continue to write
c
and
(\varepsilon_{t})_{t}
for the number and the sequence supplied by Lemma
2.14
.
Guided by Lemma
2.14
we consider the vector
\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell,t)}
of truncated log-likelihood ratios

\displaystyle\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell,t)}_{x}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-2t^{c}&\mbox{ if $x\in\partial^{2t}\mathfrak{x}$ and }{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{x}^{(\ell)}<-2t^{c}\kern 5.0pt,\\
2t^{c}&\mbox{ if $x\in\partial^{2t}\mathfrak{x}$ and }{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{x}^{(\ell)}>2t^{c}\kern 5.0pt,\\
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{x}^{(\ell)}&\mbox{ otherwise}\kern 5.0pt.\end{cases}

(7.37)

Further, let

{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell,t)}

be the result of
t
iterations of
\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}(\,\cdot\,)
starting from
\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell,t)}
.
The following corollary is a direct consequence of Lemma
7.9
and Lemma
2.14
.

Corollary 7.10
.

For any
\ell>ct^{c}
we have
{\mathbb{P}}[{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell,t)}_{\mathfrak{x}}\neq{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}}]<\varepsilon_{t}.

Proof.

Due to Lemma
2.14
, the truncation in (
7.37
) is
inconsequential with probability at least
1-\varepsilon_{t}
, in which case

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell,t)}=\mathrm{LL}^{+(t)}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell,t)})=\mathrm{LL}^{+(t)}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)})=\mathrm{LL}^{+(\ell+t)}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}(+\infty,\ldots,+\infty)={\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}\kern 5.0pt,

where the last equality follows from Lemma
7.9
.
∎

Recall that we defined the non-random operator
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{{k},d}
from (
2.17
), mimicking
\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}
.
To make the connection between the
random operator
\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}
and
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{{k},d}
precise, we introduce the
following concepts.
Given a tree formula
T
we write
V_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}(T)
, for the set of
x
variables of
T
that appear both as positive and negative literals in the sub-tree
T_{x}
comprising
x
and its the progeny.
We define
V_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}(T),V_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}(T)
, and
V_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}(T)
similarly.
Note that the above sets constitute a partition of
V(T)
.
We use
\type:V(T)\to\{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}},\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}},\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}},\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}\}
to indicate the part vertex belongs to.
We denote with
\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
the random Galton-Watson formula
\mathbb{T}
conditioned on the root
\type(\mathfrak{x})=\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}
.
We define
\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}
, and
\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}
analogously.
Degenerately, we also write
\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}
for the formula comprised by a single variable
\mathfrak{x}
.
Let us denote with
\rho^{(\ell,t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
the distribution of
\bar{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{x}}^{(\ell,t)}
in
\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
.
Moreover, let
\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
be the distribution of

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{x}}^{(\ell-t)}\cdot\mathbb{1}\left\{|{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{x}}^{(\ell-t)}|\leq 2t^{c}\right\}+2t^{c}\cdot\mathbb{1}\left\{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{x}}^{(\ell-t)}>2t^{c}\right\}-2t^{c}\cdot\mathbb{1}\left\{{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{x}}^{(\ell-t)}<-2t^{c}\right\}\kern 5.0pt,

i.e., the truncation of
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{\mathfrak{x}}^{(\ell-t)}
in

\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
.
Analogously we define the distributions
\rho^{(\ell,t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\rho^{(\ell,t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}
, and

\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}
. Notice that, degenerately,

\rho^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}=\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\Circle$}}}=\delta_{0}
.

Lemma 7.11
.

For
\ell>ct^{c}
we have that

\left(\rho^{(\ell,t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\rho^{(\ell,t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\rho^{(\ell,t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right)={\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{k,d}\left(\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right).

Proof.

We use induction on
t
. Specifically, let
\nu=\left(\nu_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\nu_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\nu_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right)
be any triplet in

\mathcal{P}(-\infty,\infty]\times\mathcal{P}[0,+\infty]\times\mathcal{P}[-\infty,0]
, and
\nu^{(t)}={\mathrm{LL}}^{\star(t)}_{k,d}(\nu)
be the outcome
of the
t
-fold application of
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{k,d}
. Moreover, let
({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{x})_{x\in V(\mathbb{T}^{(t)})}
be a vector of independent samples
with
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{x}\sim\nu_{\type(x)}
.
We claim that root value
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(t)}_{\mathfrak{x}}
of the random operator
\mathrm{LL}^{+(t)}_{{\mathbb{T}^{(t)}}}
, coincides with
\nu_{\type(\mathfrak{x})}
. Indeed, for

t=1
the claim follows readily from the definitions. For the inductive step, we notice that the
t
-fold application of
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{k,d}
is
obtained by applying
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{k,d}
to the
(t-1)
-fold application.
Per the induction hypothesis

\displaystyle\left({\mathrm{LL}^{+(t-1)}_{\mathbb{T}^{(t-1)}}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{x})_{x}}\right)_{\mathfrak{x}}\sim\nu^{(t-1)}_{\type(\mathfrak{x})}\kern 5.0pt.

(7.38)

Applying
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{k,d}
to
\nu^{(t-1)}
implies the result as the first layer of
\mathbb{T}^{(t)}
is independent of the subtrees rooted at the
grandchildren
\partial^{2}\mathfrak{x}
of the root, which are distributed as i.i.d. copies of
\mathbb{T}^{(t-1)}
.
The lemma follows from applying the above identity to

\nu=\left(\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}},\bar{\rho}^{(\ell-t)}_{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\right)
.
∎

Refining the definition of the
\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}
operator in (
1.2
), we write
\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}
for the operator obtained from

\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}
upon conditioning on
\boldsymbol{d}^{+},\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\geq 1
. Similarly
\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}
and
\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}
are obtained from

\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}
upon conditioning on
\boldsymbol{d}^{+}\geq 1,\boldsymbol{d}^{-}=0
, and
\boldsymbol{d}^{+}=0,\boldsymbol{d}^{-}\geq 1
, respectively.
We define

\displaystyle\pi^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}_{d,k}=\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}\left(\pi_{d,k}\right),\kern 5.0pt\pi^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}_{d,k}=\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}\left(\pi_{d,k}\right),\kern 5.0pt\pi^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}_{d,k}=\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}\left(\pi_{d,k}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Let us write
{\gamma},{\gamma^{-1}}
for the continuous and mutually inverse real functions

\displaystyle{\gamma}:\mathbb{R}\to(0,1),\kern 5.0ptz\mapsto(1+\tanh(z/2))/2,

\displaystyle{\gamma^{-1}}:(0,1)\to\mathbb{R},\kern 5.0ptp\mapsto\log(p/(1-p))\kern 5.0pt.

(7.39)

Let
\rho^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}_{d,k}={\gamma^{-1}}(\pi_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}})
, and define
\rho^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}_{d,k},\rho^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}_{d,k}
similarly.

Claim 7.12
.

The vector
\left({\rho^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}_{d,k},\rho^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}_{d,k},\rho^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}_{d,k}}\right)
is a fixed point of the operator
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{d,k}
.

Proof.

Let
\rho_{d,k}={\gamma^{-1}}\bigl(\pi_{d,k}\bigr)
.
First, we claim that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{d,k}\left({\rho_{d,k},\rho_{d,k},\rho_{d,k}}\right)=\left({\rho^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}}_{d,k},\rho^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}_{d,k},\rho^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}_{d,k}}\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(7.40)

Indeed, since all input distributions are the same, by Proposition
2.1
, the two summands in the left term of (
2.21
) corresponding to
\boldsymbol{d}_{+}^{\star}
and
\boldsymbol{d}_{-}^{\star}
are identically distributed, and also identically distributed to the sums that appear in the other two terms.
Therefore,
(
7.40
) follows directly from the definitions of
\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.6}{$\CIRCLE$}}},\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\oplus$}}}
, and
\mathrm{BP}_{d,k}^{\mathrel{\scalebox{0.8}{$\ominus$}}}
.
The claim now follows from (
7.40
), the definition of the operator
{\mathrm{LL}}^{\star}_{d,k}
, and the law of total probability.
∎

Let
\rho^{(\ell)}
be the distribution of the log-likelihood ratio
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}}
.

Corollary 7.13
.

For
d<d_{\mathrm{con}}(k)
the sequence
\left({{\gamma}\left({\rho^{(\ell)}}\right)}\right)_{\ell}
converges weakly to
\pi_{d,k}
.

Proof.

The result follows by combining Corollary
7.10
, Lemma
7.11
, Proposition
2.15
, Claim
7.12
,
and applying the continuous mapping theorem and the law of total probability.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.13
.

Recall that we write
\mathrm{LL}_{\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}}^{+(\ell)}
for the
\ell
-fold iteration of the operator
\mathrm{LL}^{+}_{{\mathbb{T}}^{(\ell)}}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}}=\bigl(\mathrm{LL}^{+(\ell)}_{\mathbb{T}^{(\ell)}}(0,\ldots,0)\bigr)_{\mathfrak{x}}
.
Using arguments similar to Fact
4.2
, we can show that
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}}
is nothing but the distribution of the random variable

{\gamma^{-1}}({\mathbb{P}}\left[{\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(\ell)}(\mathfrak{x})=1\mid\mathbb{T}}\right])
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{{\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(\ell)}(\mathfrak{x})=1\mid\mathbb{T}]}\sim{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})\kern 5.0pt,

and

\displaystyle{{\mathbb{P}}[\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(\ell)}(\mathfrak{x})=1\mid\forall y\in\partial^{2\ell}\mathfrak{x}:\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(\ell)}(y)=\boldsymbol{\tau}^{+}(y),\mathbb{T}]}\sim{\gamma}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})\kern 5.0pt.

Due to Lemma
2.12
,
0\leq{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})\leq{\gamma}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})\leq 1
.
Moreover, from Lemma
7.11
, Proposition
2.15
, and Claim
7.12
, we see that for
d<d_{\mathrm{con}}(k)
the sequence
\bigl({\gamma}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})\bigr)_{\ell}
converges weakly to
\pi_{d,k}
.
Finally, Corollary
7.13
implies that
\bigl({\gamma}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})\bigr)_{\ell}
also converges weakly to
\pi_{d,k}
, and thus,

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left|{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})-{\gamma}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})\right|=\lim_{\ell\to\infty}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[{{\gamma}(\boldsymbol{\theta}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{{\gamma}({\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{(\ell)}_{\mathfrak{x}})}\right]\right|=0\kern 5.0pt,

implying the assertion.
∎
